\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left\|\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}\right\|^{2}\right)+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\nabla\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|^{2}ds+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\nabla\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|^{2}ds\right]\lesssim 1,

where the hidden constants depend only on
\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T

Proof.

The thesis follows immediately combining
3.5
and assumption (
3.61
).
∎

4.
Proof of
Theorem 2.15

Let
\kappa_{\varepsilon}:=\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}\geq\eta>0
and let us introduce the following notation

\displaystyle Z^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}\left(\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}-\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{s}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}\right)dW^{k,j}_{s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}-{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}}dW^{k,j}_{s},

\displaystyle Z^{2,\varepsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}dW^{k,j}_{s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}Q\left[\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{A}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}+\partial_{3}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}}\right]dW^{k,j}_{s}.

The stochastic integrals above are well defined thanks to the regularity properties of
\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{t}},\ {{B}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime},\overline{A^{\varepsilon}_{t}},{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}
and the summability properties of the coefficients in
2.6
.
The first step in order to prove
Theorem 2.15
is to rewrite
\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}
and
\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}
in mild form. Indeed, recalling the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}
, see (
2.18
), the following lemma holds true:

Lemma 4.1
.

The following mild formulas hold true

(4.1)

\displaystyle\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}

\displaystyle=e^{t\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}+Z^{2,\varepsilon}_{t},

(4.2)

\displaystyle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}

\displaystyle=e^{t\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}+Z^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right)ds.

Proof.

Let us take
\phi=(-\nabla^{\perp}_{H}(-\Delta)^{-1}\psi_{h},0),\ \psi_{h}=e^{ih\cdot x}
as test function in (
2.4
) obtaining, since all the functions appearing in the integrals are independent from
x_{3}
, that it holds

\displaystyle\langle\overline{B^{H,\varepsilon}_{t}},-\nabla^{\perp}_{H}(-\Delta)^{-1}\psi_{h}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\overline{B^{H,\varepsilon}_{0}},-\nabla^{\perp}_{H}(-\Delta)^{-1}\psi_{h}\rangle+\kappa_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\langle\overline{B^{H,\varepsilon}_{s}},\nabla_{H}^{\perp}\psi_{h}\rangle\,ds

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}\langle\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{H,\varepsilon}_{s}}-\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{s}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{H,\varepsilon},-\nabla^{\perp}_{H}(-\Delta)^{-1}\psi_{h}\rangle dW^{k,j}_{s}

(4.3)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}\langle\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{H,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}-{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{H,\varepsilon}},-\nabla^{\perp}_{H}(-\Delta)^{-1}\psi_{h}\rangle dW^{k,j}_{s}.

Recalling that, by (
2.1
) and
3.2
, it holds

\displaystyle\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}\overline{A^{\varepsilon}_{t}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{t}}-\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{t}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{curl}(\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{A^{\varepsilon}_{t}}+D\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\overline{A^{\varepsilon}_{t}})

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}-{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{curl}(\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}+D\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}),

looking at the horizontal component, we have in particular that

(4.4)

\displaystyle\overline{B^{H,\varepsilon}_{t}}

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{H}^{\perp}\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}

(4.5)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{H,\varepsilon}_{t}}-\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{t}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{H,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\nabla_{H}^{\perp}(\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}})

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{H,\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}-{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{H,\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\nabla_{H}^{\perp}\left(\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{A}^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}+\partial_{3}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}}\right)

(4.6)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\nabla_{H}^{\perp}Q\left(\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{A}^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}+\partial_{3}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}}\right).

Now both
\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}
an
Q\left(\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{A}^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}+\partial_{3}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{t}}^{\prime}}\right)
are zero mean. Substituting (
4.4
), (
4.5
), (
4.6
) in (
4.3
) and integrating by parts we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\psi_{h}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}},\psi_{h}\rangle-\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\langle\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}},\psi_{h}\rangle\,ds

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}\langle\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}},\psi_{h}\rangle dW^{k,j}_{s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}\langle Q\left[\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{A}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}+\partial_{3}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}}\right],\psi_{h}\rangle dW^{k,j}_{s}.

Therefore, applying Itô formula to
e^{t\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}}\langle\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\psi_{h}\rangle
we have

\displaystyle\langle\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\psi_{h}\rangle

\displaystyle=e^{-t\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}}\langle\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}},\psi_{h}\rangle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}}\langle\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}},\psi_{h}\rangle dW^{k,j}_{s}

(4.7)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}}\langle Q\left[\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{A}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}+\partial_{3}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}}\right],\psi_{h}\rangle dW^{k,j}_{s}\quad\mathbb{P}-a.s.\quad\forall t\in[0,T].

We can find
\Gamma\subseteq\Omega
of full probability such that the above equality holds for all
t\in[0,T]
and all
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}
. But this is exactly (
4.1
) written in Fourier modes.

Let us consider for
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}

\phi=(0,0,\psi_{h})^{t},\ \psi_{h}=e^{ih\cdot x}
as test function in (
2.4
) obtaining, since all the functions appearing in the integrals are independent from
x_{3}
, that it holds

\displaystyle\langle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\psi_{h}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}},\psi_{h}\rangle-\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\langle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}},\psi_{h}\rangle\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right),\psi_{h}\rangle ds

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}\langle\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}-\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{s}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon},\psi_{h}\rangle dW^{k,j}_{s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}\langle\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}-{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}},\psi_{h}\rangle dW^{k,j}_{s}.

Therefore, applying Itô formula to
e^{t\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}}\langle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\psi_{h}\rangle
we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\psi_{h}\rangle

\displaystyle=e^{-t\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}}\langle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}},\psi_{h}\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}}\langle\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right),\psi_{h}\rangle ds

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}}\langle\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}-\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{s}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon},\psi_{h}\rangle dW^{k,j}_{s}

(4.8)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\lvert h\rvert^{2}}\langle\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}-{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}},\psi_{h}\rangle dW^{k,j}_{s}\quad\mathbb{P}-a.s.\quad\forall t\in[0,T].

We can then find
\Gamma\subseteq\Omega
of full probability such that the above equality holds for all
t\in[0,T]
and all
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}
. But this is exactly (
4.2
) written in Fourier modes.
∎

Secondly we need to study some properties of the stochastic convolutions
Z^{1,\varepsilon}_{t},Z^{2,\varepsilon}_{t}
.
4.2
and
4.3
below are the analogous of
[
3
, Lemma 2.5]
in our framework. In particular it is important to point out that
[
3
, Assumption 2.4]
is false in our case and we have to deal also with the stochastic stretching, which was neglected in previous results.

Lemma 4.2
.

For each
\delta>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
large enough such that
3.5
holds, then

(4.9)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\delta}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

(4.10)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z^{2,\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\delta}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

The hidden constants in the inequality above depend only on
\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T
.

Proof.

By Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality and
2.1
we have for each
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\delta}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\lvert\rho\rvert}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}\left(\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}-\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{s}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\delta}}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}-{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\delta}}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\left\|\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1}}^{2}+\left\|\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{s}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\left\|\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1}}^{2}+\left\|\overline{{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}}\right\|^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\left\|\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{T}^{3}_{\varepsilon})}^{2}+\left\|\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{s}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\left\|\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{T}^{3}_{\varepsilon})}^{2}+\left\|\overline{{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}ds\right]

(4.11)

\displaystyle=V^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}+V^{2,\varepsilon}_{t}+V^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}+V^{4,\varepsilon}_{t}.

Now let us estimate
V^{1,\varepsilon}_{t},\ V^{2,\varepsilon}_{t},\ V^{3,\varepsilon}_{t},\ V^{4,\varepsilon}_{t}
. First we have by Hölder inequality since the
\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}
are divergence free

\displaystyle V^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}+V^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj=1\end{subarray}}{\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\left\|\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|^{2}_{\varepsilon}ds}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}{\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\left\|\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}_{\varepsilon}ds}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta,C_{1,H},C_{V}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left({\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|^{2}_{\varepsilon}\right]ds}+\frac{1}{N^{\beta-2}}{\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}_{\varepsilon}\right]ds\right)

(4.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T}\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

where in the last step we exploit (
3.39
) and (
3.41
). For what concerns the other two terms, noting that if
k_{3}=0
then
\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}\neq 0
if and only if
j=2
, thanks to Holder inequality and (
3.38
), (
3.40
) we get

\displaystyle V^{2,\varepsilon}_{t}+V^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta,\gamma,C_{2,H},C_{V}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N^{\gamma-4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\overline{B^{H,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{N^{\beta-4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}_{\varepsilon}\right]ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N^{\gamma-4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{N^{\beta-4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}_{\varepsilon}\right]ds

(4.13)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

Combining (
4
), (
4
), (
4
) relation (
4.9
) follows. The proof of (
4.10
) is similar and we omit the details.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

For each
\delta>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
large enough such that
3.5
holds, then

(4.14)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-2\delta}}^{2}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta^{2}N^{2}}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

(4.15)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z^{2,\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-2\delta}}^{2}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta^{2}N^{2}}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

where the hidden constants depend only on
\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T
.

Proof.

By Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality and
2.1
we have for each
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-2\delta}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\lvert\rho\rvert}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}\left(\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}-\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{s}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-2\delta}}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\kappa_{\varepsilon}\Delta}\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}-{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-2\delta}}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\left\|\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-2-\delta}}^{2}+\left\|\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{s}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-2\delta}}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}{\left\|\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1}}^{2}}+\left\|\overline{{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-2\delta}}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\left\|\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-2-\delta}}^{2}+\left\|\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{s}}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-2\delta}}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\overline{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla{{B}^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{T}^{3}_{\varepsilon})}^{2}+\left\|\overline{{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-2\delta}}^{2}ds\right]

(4.16)

\displaystyle=Y^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}+Y^{2,\varepsilon}_{t}+Y^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}+Y^{4,\varepsilon}_{t}.

The term
Y^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}
can be simply treated as
V^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}
in the proof of
4.2
obtaining

(4.17)

\displaystyle Y^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}\lesssim_{\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T}\frac{1}{\varepsilon N^{\beta-2}}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

Let us consider now the others which are the more difficult ones. We will use in the following, even if not specified later, that all the vector fields considered are divergence free, moreover for
k_{3}=0
we
\sigma_{k,j}^{H,\varepsilon}\neq 0
if and only if
j=1
and
\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}\neq 0
if and only if
j=2
. Let us start considering
Y^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}
obtaining, thanks to the definition of Sobolev norms in the two dimensional torus

\displaystyle Y^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\left\|\sigma_{k,j}^{H,\varepsilon}\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-\delta}}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\left\|e^{ik\cdot x}\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-1-\delta}}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi N^{2}}{\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}}\frac{1}{\lvert j\rvert^{2+2\delta}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}e^{ik\cdot x},e^{-ij\cdot x}\rangle^{2}\right]ds}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi N^{2}}{\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}},e^{-ik\cdot x}\rangle^{2}\right]\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}}\frac{1}{\lvert j\rvert^{2+2\delta}}ds}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta N^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\delta}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|^{2}\right]ds

(4.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T}\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta^{2}N^{2}}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

where in the last step we exploited (
3.39
). Let us consider
Y^{2,\varepsilon}_{t}
obtaining, thanks to (
3.25
)

\displaystyle Y^{2,\varepsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0,\hskip 8.19447ptj=2\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}\overline{B^{H,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{2,H}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}\\
N\leq\lvert k\rvert\leq 2N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\lvert k\rvert^{\gamma}}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\overline{B^{H,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}\frac{1}{N^{\gamma-2}}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|^{2}\right]ds

(4.19)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T}\frac{1}{\varepsilon N^{\gamma-2}}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

where in the last step we exploited (
3.38
). Lastly we treat
Y^{4,\varepsilon}_{t}
, obtaining thanks to (
3.8
) and the fact that
\operatorname{div}
and
M^{\varepsilon}
commute

\displaystyle Y^{4,\varepsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\overline{{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}}\right\|^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\overline{{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}\neq 0,\hskip 8.19447ptj\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\sigma_{k,j}^{3,\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta,C_{V}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon N^{\beta-2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{{B}^{\varepsilon}_{s}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}ds\right]

(4.20)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T}\frac{1}{\varepsilon N^{\beta-2}}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

Relation (
4.14
) follows combining (
4
), (
4.17
), (
4
), (
4
), (
4
). The proof of (
4.15
) is similar and we omit the details.
∎

Interpolating between
4.2
and
4.3
we obtain:

Corollary 4.4
.

For each
\theta\in(0,1]
and
\delta\in(0,\theta]
it holds

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta}}^{2}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta^{\frac{1+\theta}{1+\delta}}N^{\frac{2(\theta-\delta)}{1+\delta}}}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z^{2,\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta}}^{2}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta^{\frac{1+\theta}{1+\delta}}N^{\frac{2(\theta-\delta)}{1+\delta}}}\left({\left\|B_{0}^{3,\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+{\left\|{{A}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

The hidden constants in the inequality above depend only on
\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T
.

Moreover, we recall that by classical theory of evolution equations, see for example
[
15
]
, or arguing as in
4.1
, the unique weak solutions of (
2.32
), (
2.31
) can be written in mild form as

\displaystyle\overline{A_{t}^{3,\gamma}}=e^{t(\eta+\eta_{T})\Delta}\overline{A^{3}_{0}},\quad\overline{B_{t}^{3,\gamma}}=e^{t(\eta+\eta_{T})\Delta}\overline{B^{3}_{0}}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta_{T})\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}\overline{A^{3}_{s}}\right)ds.

Now we introduce some intermediate functions between
(\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}})
and
(\overline{B_{t}^{3}},\overline{A_{t}^{3}})
. Let
\left(\widehat{B_{t}^{3,\varepsilon}},\widehat{A_{t}^{3,\varepsilon}}\right)
the unique weak solution of the linear system

(4.21)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}&=(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}\quad x\in\mathbb{T}^{2},\ t\in(0,T)\\
\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}|_{t=0}&=\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}},\end{cases}

(4.22)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\widehat{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}&=(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta\widehat{B^{3,\gamma}_{t}}+\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}\right)\quad x\in\mathbb{T}^{2},\ t\in(0,T)\\
\widehat{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}|_{t=0}&=\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}.\end{cases}

Again, by classical theory of evolution equations, see for example
[
15
]
, or arguing as in
4.1
, the unique weak solutions of (
4.21
), (
4.22
) can be written in mild form as

\displaystyle\widehat{A_{t}^{3,\varepsilon}}=e^{t(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta}\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}},\quad\widehat{B_{t}^{3,\varepsilon}}=e^{t(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta}\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta_{T}^{\varepsilon})\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right)ds.

Now we prove that
(\widehat{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}})

and
(\overline{B^{3,\gamma}_{t}},\overline{A^{3,\gamma}_{t}})
are close.

Lemma 4.5
.

Under the same assumptions of
Theorem 2.15
, for each
\theta_{1}\in(0,1],\ \theta_{2}\in(0,\theta_{1})
we have

(4.23)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}-\overline{A^{3,\gamma}_{t}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,T}\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert^{\theta_{2}/2}+\left\|\overline{v^{3}_{0}}-\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}},

(4.24)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\widehat{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}-\overline{B^{3,\gamma}_{t}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,T}\begin{cases}\frac{\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert^{\theta_{2}/2}+\left\|\overline{A^{3}_{0}}-\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}}{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}+\left\|\overline{B^{3}_{0}}-\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\theta_{1}N}\\
\text{if }\gamma=4,\\
\frac{\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert^{\theta_{2}/2}+\left\|\overline{A^{3}_{0}}-\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}}{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}+\left\|\overline{B^{3}_{0}}-\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\theta_{1}N^{\gamma/2-2}}\\
\text{if }\gamma>4.\end{cases}

Proof.

First let us observe that by assumptions

\displaystyle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\rightharpoonup\overline{B^{3}_{0}}\text{ in }\dot{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}),\quad\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\rightharpoonup\overline{A^{3}_{0}}\text{ in }\dot{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})

we have in particular that the families
\left\{\left\|\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|^{2}\right\}_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)},\ \left\{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|^{2}\right\}_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}
are bounded. Therefore

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\frac{{\left\|\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}{\varepsilon}=\operatorname{sup}_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}{\left\|\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|}^{2}<+\infty,\quad\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\rightarrow\overline{B^{3}_{0}}\text{ in }\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\quad\forall\theta_{2}>0,

(4.25)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\frac{{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}{\varepsilon}=\operatorname{sup}_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|}^{2}<+\infty,\quad\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\rightarrow\overline{A^{3}_{0}}\text{ in }\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\quad\forall\theta_{2}>0.

Moreover, since by
2.10

\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon},
then in particular we have

(4.26)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\left\|\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\right\|_{HS}<+\infty.

Lastly, by simple energy estimates on (
4.21
) we have for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1)

(4.27)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}\right\|^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\nabla\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|^{2}ds\lesssim_{\eta}\operatorname{sup}_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}{\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|}^{2}<+\infty.

The convergence of
\widehat{A^{\varepsilon,3}_{t}}
to
\overline{A^{3,\gamma}_{t}}
then follows by triangle inequality,
2.1
and (
4
). Indeed for each
t\in[0,T]
it holds

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}-\overline{A^{3,\gamma}_{t}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(e^{t(\eta+\eta_{T}^{\varepsilon})\Delta}-e^{t(\eta+\eta_{T})\Delta}\right)\overline{A^{3}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}+\left\|e^{t(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta}\left(\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}-\overline{A^{3}_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,T}\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert^{\theta_{2}/2}\left\|\overline{A^{3}_{0}}\right\|+\left\|\overline{A^{3}_{0}}-\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert^{\theta_{2}/2}+\left\|\overline{A^{3}_{0}}-\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}.

Thanks to (
4.23
) we can obtain also (
4.24
). Indeed thanks to triangle inequality for each
t\in[0,T]
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}-\overline{B^{3,\gamma}_{t}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left\|\left(e^{t(\eta+\eta_{T}^{\varepsilon})\Delta}-e^{t(\eta+\eta_{T})\Delta}\right)\overline{B^{3}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}+\left\|e^{t(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta}\left(\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}-\overline{B^{3}_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\left(e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta_{T}^{\varepsilon})\Delta}-e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta_{T})\Delta}\right)\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right)ds\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta_{T})\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}\right)\nabla^{\perp}\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right)ds\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta_{T})\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}-\nabla^{\perp}\overline{A^{3,\gamma}_{s}}\right)\right)ds\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

I_{1}
can be treated as above obtaining:

(4.28)

\displaystyle I_{1}\lesssim_{\eta,T}\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert^{\theta_{1}/2}+\left\|\overline{B^{3}_{0}}-\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}.

Thanks to
2.1
, (
4.26
) and (
4.27
) it holds

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert^{\theta_{1}/2}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\theta_{1}/2}\left\|e^{(t-s)\eta\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right)\right\|ds

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta}\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert^{\theta_{1}/2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\theta_{1}/2}}\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|ds

(4.29)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\frac{1}{\theta_{1}}\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert^{\theta_{1}/2}.

Due to
2.1
and (
4.27
) we can treat
I_{3}
obtaining:

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,\theta_{1}}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\theta_{1}/2}}\left\|\operatorname{div}\left(\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}\right)\nabla^{\perp}\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right)\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-2}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}\right\|_{HS}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\theta_{1}/2}}\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|ds

(4.30)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\frac{1}{\theta_{1}}\left\|\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma}\right\|_{HS}.

In particular, combining (
4
) and
2.10
we have that

(4.31)

\displaystyle I_{3}\lesssim_{\eta,T}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\theta_{1}N}&\text{if }\gamma=4,\\
\frac{1}{\theta_{1}N^{\gamma/2-2}}&\text{if }\gamma>4.\end{cases}

Lastly we treat
I_{4}
combining
2.1
and (
4.23
):

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1+\theta_{2}/2-\theta_{1}/2}}\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}-\overline{A^{3,\gamma}_{s}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\frac{1}{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}-\overline{A^{3,\gamma}_{t}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}

(4.32)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,T}\frac{\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert^{\theta_{2}/2}+\left\|\overline{A^{3}_{0}}-\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}}{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}.

Combining (
4.28
),(
4
),(
4.31
),(
4
) we get the thesis.
∎

Secondly we provide a quantitative result on the closeness of

(\widehat{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}})

and
(\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}).

Lemma 4.6
.

Under the same assumptions of
Theorem 2.15
, there exists
N_{0}
large enough such that for each
N\geq N_{0}
, for each
\theta_{1}\in(0,1],\ \theta_{2}\in(0,\theta_{1}),\ \delta\in(0,\theta_{2})
we have

(4.33)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}-\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}\right\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta^{\frac{1+\theta_{2}}{1+\delta}}N^{\frac{2(\theta_{2}-\delta)}{1+\delta}}},

(4.34)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widehat{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}-\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\theta_{2}^{2-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}\delta^{\frac{(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})(1+\theta_{2})}{2(1+\delta)}}N^{\frac{(\theta_{2}-\delta)(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})}{(1+\delta)}}},

where the hidden constants depend only on
\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T
.

Proof.

From
4.1
, we already know that
\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}
and
\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}
can be rewritten in mild form as

\displaystyle\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}=e^{t(\eta+\eta_{T}^{\varepsilon})\Delta}\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}+Z_{t}^{1,\varepsilon}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right)ds,\quad\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}=e^{t(\eta+\eta_{T}^{\varepsilon})\Delta}\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{0}}+Z_{t}^{2,\varepsilon}.

Therefore denoting by

\displaystyle D^{\varepsilon}_{t}=\overline{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}-\widehat{B^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}},\quad d^{\varepsilon}_{t}=\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}-\overline{A^{3}_{t}},

we have

\displaystyle D^{\varepsilon}_{t}=Z_{t}^{1,\varepsilon}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}d^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right)ds,\quad d^{\varepsilon}_{s}=Z_{t}^{2,\varepsilon}.

Due to
4.4
we have immediately (
4.33
). Now we move to the analysis of
D^{\varepsilon}_{t}
.
Z^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}
can be treated easily by
4.4
obtaining

(4.35)

\displaystyle\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z^{1,\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T}\frac{1}{\delta^{\frac{1+\theta_{1}}{1+\delta}}N^{\frac{2(\theta_{1}-\delta)}{1+\delta}}}.

In order to treat the deterministic convolution, we first argue by interpolation obtaining thanks to
2.1
, (
4.26
)

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}d^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right)ds\right\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}d^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right)ds\right\|^{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}_{\dot{H}^{-2-\theta_{2}}}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}d^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right)ds\right\|^{2-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left\|d^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}ds\right)^{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{1-\theta_{2}/2}}\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}-\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|ds\right)^{2-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}

(4.36)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\frac{1}{\theta_{2}^{2-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left\|d^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{2}}\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}\right\|+\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|\right)^{2-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}.

Combining (
4
) and (
4.33
), (
3.6
), (
4.27
) we obtain an estimate of the deterministic convolution. Indeed, by Holder inequality, for each
t\in[0,T]
it holds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)(\eta+\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T})\Delta}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\nabla^{\perp}d^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right)ds\right\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{1}}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,T}\frac{1}{\theta_{2}^{2-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left\|d^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{2}}\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}\right\|+\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|\right)^{2-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\theta_{2}^{2-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left\|d^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{-\theta_{2}}}^{2}ds\right]^{\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left\|\overline{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{t}}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\widehat{A^{3,\varepsilon}_{s}}\right\|^{2}\right)\right]^{\frac{2-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}{2}}

(4.37)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\eta,\beta,\gamma,C_{V},C_{1,H},C_{2,H},\lvert\rho\rvert,T}\frac{1}{\theta_{2}^{2-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}\delta^{\frac{(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})(1+\theta_{2})}{2(1+\delta)}}N^{\frac{(\theta_{2}-\delta)(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})}{(1+\delta)}}}.

Combining (
4.35
) and (
4
) the thesis follows.
∎

Now we are ready to prove
Theorem 2.15
.

Proof of
Theorem 2.15
.

Recalling that, due to the properties of Riemann sums,

\displaystyle\lvert\eta^{\varepsilon}_{T}-\eta_{T}\rvert\lesssim\frac{1}{N},

then the thesis follows immediately by triangle inequality combining
4.5
and
4.6
.
∎

Appendix A
Proof of
2.12

We start showing that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}}F

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\varepsilon}F.

First let us observe that it holds

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}}F

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{div}(\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\otimes\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}\nabla F)-R

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}\left(Q^{\varepsilon}_{0}(0)\nabla F\right)-R

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\varepsilon}F-R,

where we denoted by

\displaystyle R=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\left(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}\right)+\left(\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}-(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}.

Therefore, it is enough to show that

(A.1)

\displaystyle R=0.

First we observe that

\displaystyle\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\left(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}\right)=(\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}+\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla^{2}\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}F.

Therefore

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}(\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}+\left(\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\right)+\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla^{2}\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}F-(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\right)

(A.2)

\displaystyle=R_{1}+R_{2}.

R_{1}
is easily
0
due to (
2.16
). In order to show that
R_{2}=0
, we observe that, recalling the definition of
\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}
and expanding the sum, it holds for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0},\ j\in\{1,2\}

\displaystyle\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla^{2}\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}F-(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=-2\sum_{l,m\in\{1,2,3\}}\left\lvert\theta_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\right\rvert^{2}a_{k,j}k_{l}k_{m}(a_{k,j})_{l}F_{m}

\displaystyle=2\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla^{2}\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}F

(A.3)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{m\in\{1,2,3\}}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\partial_{m}\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}F_{m}.

Let us show that for each
m\in\{1,2,3\}
it holds
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\partial_{m}\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}=0
.
Due to the fact that
\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}
are divergence free, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\partial_{m}\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\otimes\partial_{m}\sigma_{-k,j}^{\varepsilon}\right)

(A.4)

\displaystyle=i\operatorname{div}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\left\lvert\theta_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}\right\rvert^{2}k_{m}a_{k,j}\otimes a_{k,j}\right)=0.

Our claim (
A.1
) then follows combining (
A
), (
A
), (
A
).

Now let us prove that

\displaystyle\rho\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}}+\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}}\right)F

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}F.

First let us observe that it holds

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}}+\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}}\right)F

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}\operatorname{div}\left((\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\otimes\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}+\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\otimes\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon})\nabla F\right)-\tilde{R}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}\left(\overline{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\rho}}(0)\nabla F\right)-\tilde{R},

where we denoted by

\displaystyle\tilde{R}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\left(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}\right)+\left(\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}-(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\left(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}\right)+\left(\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}-(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}.

Since
\overline{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\rho}}(0)=0
, we are left to show that

(A.5)

\displaystyle-\rho\tilde{R}=\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{\rho}F.

First we observe that

\displaystyle\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\left(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}\right)+\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\left(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}+\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla^{2}\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}F

\displaystyle+\left(\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}+\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla^{2}\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}F.

Therefore

\displaystyle\tilde{R}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}\left(\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}+\left(\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}+\left(\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}+\left(\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla^{2}-(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}F+\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla^{2}\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}F-(F\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\right)

(A.6)

\displaystyle=\tilde{R}_{1}+\tilde{R}_{2}.

\tilde{R}_{2}=0
arguing as above when showing that
R_{2}=0.
We have for each
k
such that
k_{3}=0

(A.7)

\displaystyle\left(\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}=\left(\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon},\quad\left(\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}=\left(\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}

due to our choice of the
\sigma_{k,j}^{\varepsilon}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\tilde{R}=2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}\left(\sigma_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,2}^{\varepsilon}+\left(\sigma_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla F\right)\cdot\nabla\sigma_{-k,1}^{\varepsilon}.

Since, due to (
2.14
), we have for each
l\in\{1,2,3\}

\displaystyle\partial_{l}\overline{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\rho}}(0)=2i\rho\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}\left(\theta_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}{\theta_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}}^{*}a_{k,1}\otimes a_{k,2}+\theta_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}{\theta_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}}^{*}a_{k,2}\otimes a_{k,1}\right)k_{l},

it follows that

(A.8)

\displaystyle-\rho\tilde{R}=-\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\partial_{l}\overline{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\rho}}(0)\cdot\nabla F_{l}.

Relation (
A.5
) then is a consequence of (
A
), (
A.7
), (
A.8
).

Remark A.1
.

Due to
2.12
, recalling the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}
in (
2.18
), it follows that if
F:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}F

\displaystyle=-\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}(0,0,\partial_{l}\overline{Q^{\varepsilon,3,H}_{\rho}}(0)\cdot\nabla_{H}F_{l})^{t}

\displaystyle=2\rho\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}(0,0,\theta_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\theta_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}k_{l}a_{k,1}\cdot\nabla_{H}F_{l})^{t}

\displaystyle=2\rho\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,N}_{0}\\
k_{3}=0\end{subarray}}(0,0,\theta_{k,1}^{\varepsilon}\theta_{k,2}^{\varepsilon}\operatorname{div}\left(k_{l}a_{k,1}F_{l}\right))^{t}

\displaystyle=-\left(0,0,\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}F_{H}\right)\right)^{t}.

More in general if
F:\mathbb{T}^{3}_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{\rho}F

\displaystyle=-\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}(\partial_{l}\overline{Q^{\varepsilon,H,3}_{\rho}}(0)\partial_{3}F_{l},\partial_{l}\overline{Q^{\varepsilon,3,H}_{\rho}}(0)\cdot\nabla_{H}F_{l})^{t}

\displaystyle=-\left(\sum_{l\in\{1,2\}}\partial_{3}(\partial_{l}\overline{Q^{\varepsilon,H,3}_{\rho}}(0)F_{l}),\operatorname{div}_{H}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}F_{H}\right)\right)^{t}.

Appendix B
Some physical remarks

In this section we discuss some physical properties of our random vector field connected with the appearance of the alpha-term, in particular we show that the helicity of our vector field approach a constant value in the limit, despite the noise is weakly converging to zero.
Let us compute the time average of the helicity of our random velocity field (in the following we will omit the dependence on time and space)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon,\gamma}:=\frac{1}{2T}\mathbb{E}\left[W^{\varepsilon}(T,x)\cdot(\grad\times W^{\varepsilon}(T,x))\right].

As before we split our noise in mean and fluctuations along the thin direction
W^{\varepsilon}=\overline{W^{\varepsilon}}+{W^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}
, and split the mean part in horizontal and vertical components
\overline{W^{\varepsilon}}=\overline{W^{\varepsilon,H}}+\overline{W^{\varepsilon,3}}e_{3}
. Observe that

\displaystyle\grad\times\overline{W^{\varepsilon}}=(-\grad^{\perp}\overline{W^{\varepsilon,3}},\grad^{\perp}\cdot\overline{W^{\varepsilon,H}}),

where with a small abuse of notation we have indicated with
\overline{W^{\varepsilon,3}}
the scalar quantity
\overline{W^{\varepsilon}}\cdot e_{3}

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon,\gamma}=\frac{1}{2T}\mathbb{E}\left[-\overline{W^{\varepsilon,H}}\cdot\grad^{\perp}\overline{W^{\varepsilon,3}}+\overline{W^{\varepsilon,3}}\grad^{\perp}\cdot\overline{W^{\varepsilon,H}}+{W^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}\cdot(\grad\times\overline{W^{\varepsilon}})+\overline{W^{\varepsilon}}\cdot(\grad\times{W^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime})+{W^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}\cdot(\grad\times{W^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime})\right].

Notice that the mixed terms are zero by the independence of the Brownian motions, thus we are left with the first two terms, and the last one. We start by the first two, we have, by our choices of the noise coefficients,

\displaystyle-\overline{W^{\varepsilon,H}}\cdot\grad^{\perp}\overline{W^{\varepsilon,3}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0},\\
N\leq|k|,|k^{\prime}|\leq 2N\end{subarray}}\theta^{\varepsilon}_{k,1}\theta^{\varepsilon}_{k^{\prime},2}a_{k}^{1}\cdot(i{k^{\prime}}^{\perp})e^{i(k+k^{\prime})\cdot x}W^{k,1}W^{k^{\prime},2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{C_{1,H}C_{2,H}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0},\\
N\leq|k|,|k^{\prime}|\leq 2N\end{subarray}}\frac{k^{\perp}\cdot(k^{\prime})^{\perp}}{|k|^{2}|k^{\prime}|^{\gamma/2}}e^{i(k+k^{\prime})\cdot x}W^{k,1}W^{k^{\prime},2}.

Taking the expected value and remembering that
\mathbb{E}\left[W_{T}^{k,1}W_{T}^{k^{\prime},2}\right]=2T\rho\delta_{k+k^{\prime}}
we get

\mathbb{E}\left[-\overline{W^{\varepsilon,H}}\cdot\grad^{\perp}\overline{W^{\varepsilon,3}}\right]=-\frac{2\rho T}{C_{1,H}C_{2,H}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}\\
N\leq|k|\leq 2N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|k|^{\gamma/2}}.

Analogously, we have

\displaystyle\overline{W^{\varepsilon,3}}(\grad^{\perp}\cdot\overline{W^{\varepsilon,H}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{C_{1,H}C_{2,H}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0},\\
N\leq|k|,|k^{\prime}|\leq 2N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|k|^{\gamma/2}}e^{i(k+k^{\prime})\cdot x}W^{k,2}W^{k^{\prime},1}.

From which

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{W^{\varepsilon,3}}(\grad^{\perp}\cdot\overline{W^{\varepsilon,H}})\right]=-\frac{2\rho T}{C_{1,H}C_{2,H}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{0}\\
N\leq|k|\leq 2N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|k|^{\gamma/2}}.

Finally for the last term

\displaystyle{W^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}\cdot(\grad\times{W^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3,\varepsilon}_{0},\\
k_{3},k^{\prime}_{3}\neq 0\\
N\leq|k_{H}|,|k_{H}^{\prime}|\leq 2N\\
i,j\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\theta^{\varepsilon}_{i,k}\theta^{\varepsilon}_{k,k^{\prime}}a_{k}^{i}\cdot(ik^{\prime}\times a_{k^{\prime}}^{j})e^{i(k+k^{\prime})\cdot x}W^{k,i}W^{k^{\prime},j}.

Taking expectation, we see that only the terms with
k^{\prime}=-k
and
i=j
survive but then,
a_{k}^{i}\cdot(k\times a_{-k}^{i})=a_{k}^{i}\cdot(k\times a_{k}^{i})=0
.
In the end
\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon,\gamma}
does not vanish in the limit if and only if
\gamma=4
. Indeed, we have

(B.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon,\gamma}=-\frac{2\rho\zeta^{N}_{H,\gamma/2}}{N^{\gamma/2-2}C_{1,H}C_{2,H}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}=\begin{cases}-\frac{4\pi\rho\log 2}{C_{1,H}C_{2,H}}&\text{if }\gamma=4\\
0&\text{if }\gamma>4.\end{cases}

Due to (
B.1
) it follows that

(B.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}=-\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon,\gamma}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix},\ \mathcal{R}_{\gamma}=-\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}.

Finally, due to (
B.2
), denoting by

\displaystyle\overline{B^{\gamma}_{t}}=(-\nabla^{\perp}\overline{A^{3,\gamma}_{t}},\overline{B^{3,\gamma}_{t}})^{t},\quad\mathcal{A}^{\gamma}=-\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&0\end{bmatrix},

our limit equations (
2.31
), (
2.32
) read as

(B.3)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\overline{B^{\gamma}_{t}}&=(\eta+\eta_{T})\Delta\overline{B^{\gamma}_{t}}+\nabla\times(\mathcal{A}^{\gamma}\overline{B^{\gamma}_{t}})\quad x\in\mathbb{T}^{2},\ t\in(0,T)\\
\operatorname{div}\overline{B^{\gamma}_{t}}&=0\\
\overline{B^{\gamma}_{t}}|_{t=0}&=\overline{B}_{0}.\end{cases}
